Data Intuitive · Application SBOM

Application SBOM

nichecompass gene_program_mask

Generation of a prior knowledge gene program mask for NicheCompass. The core consists of the declared component script, the language runtime, the 2 directly loaded packages nichecompass and pandas and the 1 local resource setup_logger. Linked dependencies are accounted for separately. The full inventory is not a list of software proven to have executed.

4Application that performs the task

ComponentVersion or identityRole and basis
Component script script.py
SHA-256 ee96228d90fa0fd8a4b021834eba603a7f9bbd42c37b027ec390bcc9c8d16e8d		 Y declares it as a python_script resource. P defines 6 functions and calls main() under the __main__ guard.
Python 3.11.11 The python runtime, located by J at /opt/conda/bin/python3.11. The start command itself is not recorded.
nichecompass 0.3.3 P loads nichecompass.utils (5 names). J holds the package record.
pandas 2.3.3 P loads pandas as pd. J holds the package record.
setup_logger not stated P imports setup_logger. Y lists it as a resource. The module body is not part of these inputs.
Standard library with the runtime P loads json, os, pathlib, shutil, sys, which ship with the runtime. No separate package record.

Functional execution

After the imports and logger setup, main() validates its parameters and runs the enabled stages in the order Omnipath, NicheNet, MeBocost, CollecTRI TF. It then combines and filters the results and writes the output file. Y sets every stage option to its declared default; that does not establish which settings any given run used.

The block between the Viash start and end markers in P holds development values and is replaced at build time. It is not a recorded runtime profile.

The external loads sit before any conditional stage, so they happen on every run.

The code of the loaded libraries and the generated entry point are not part of these inputs.

5Linked application dependencies

From nichecompass, J reaches 176 other components through its dependsOn edges. 9 of them are direct relations, listed below; 166 are further relations held in the register. This selection accounts for dependencies. It does not assert that every package is loaded or executed.

Transitive dependencies

The remaining 166 appear in SBOM_selection_register.csv under category D2 with their exact versions, locations and a traceable chain. Examples read from J: nichecompass -> decoupler -> adjusttext; nichecompass -> mlflow -> aiohttp; nichecompass -> mlflow -> alembic. These are package relations, not evidence of a started process or an executed test.

Why this selection is not an execution list

The internal functions of the loaded libraries and the body of the local resources are not among these inputs. J carries no function or import trace, and records no condition on these edges from which use inside this script could be proven. The linked dependencies are therefore relevant to the assessment, with the evidence status dependency relation present.

Relations that must not be filled in

pyg-lib, torch, torch-scatter, torch-sparseare present and are named explicitly in the build recipe, but J connects none of them by a path from nichecompass. They are neither added silently to the application chain nor marked unused. Their place is the platform overview below.

Verifiability of the selection

The 376 name-version combinations cover all 501 package records in J. Where a combination carries more than one record, the strongest demonstrated link decides its category; the register keeps every bom-ref and location. The dependency graph was followed on exact bom-ref. The original sbom.json remains the complete inventory.

Conclusion

The application tables hold the demonstrably linked code, loads and runtime. The dependency table and the register account for the wider package chain. Operating system and other environment are managed separately. These sources do not support a final statement that software placed outside the application tables is not executed.

What each blank column is for

Intended use. What this application relies on the item to do, and the functional and performance requirements that follow from it. Anomaly list. Whether the item's published defect list has been reviewed against that intended use, with the date and the version reviewed. Risk. What could go wrong if the item behaves incorrectly in this application, and what mitigates it. Verification. How correct behaviour was confirmed here, referencing the test record rather than repeating it.

These four are judgements about this application. An SBOM records what is present, never what it is for, so no tool can fill them and none should appear to.

Qualified execution environment

This record covers the contents of the container. It says nothing about where that container ran, and a container behaves differently on a machine that was never qualified. Those facts belong to the infrastructure record, referenced here.

Host or infrastructure
Orchestrator and version
Container runtime and version
Infrastructure qualification reference

Traceability to the validation package

An inventory is not validation. It is one input to it, and is only usable as evidence when it can be tied to the requirements it satisfies and the tests that demonstrated it.

Validation plan

Test protocol (IQ, OQ, PQ)

Related records held separately

Known vulnerabilities, VEX statements and licence obligations are deliberately not in this document: an inventory is immutable for a given digest and an advisory list is not. They are separate dated records against the same digest, identified here by name and hash.

Record integrity

The three inputs are identified by SHA-256 under Identity and sources, and the generator and its version are printed below. Regenerating from those inputs reproduces this document, so the record is verifiable rather than merely asserted.

What that does not cover. It does not establish who ran the generator or when, beyond the timestamp it wrote; it does not protect the file after generation; and it is not an electronic signature. Attribution, access control, audit trail and retention are properties of the system this record is filed in, not of the file.
